```
     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It allows configuring builds via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping.
     8→
     9→## Build Commands
    10→
    11→Ensure a recent tspec is installed:
    12→```bash
    13→cargo install --path .         # Install from local source
    14→```
    15→
    16→Development workflow:
    17→```bash
    18→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests
    19→tspec clean                    # Clean build artifacts
    20→tspec clippy                   # Run lints
    21→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting
    22→```
    23→
    24→## Architecture
    25→
    26→### Execute Trait Pattern
    27→
    28→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    29→
    30→```rust
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    37→```
    38→
    39→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    40→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    41→
    42→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    43→```rust
    44→enum Commands {
    45→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    46→    Test(TestCmd),
    47→    // ...
    48→}
    49→```
    50→
    51→### Key Modules
    52→
    53→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    54→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, execute_cargo_subcommand helper, re-exports
    55→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    56→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    57→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    58→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing
    59→- `cargo_build.rs` - Build orchestration with spec application
    60→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace member discovery
    61→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    62→
    63→### Translation Spec Structure
    64→
    65→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    66→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    67→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    68→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    69→
    70→## Conventions
    71→
    72→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    73→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    74→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-crate projects; tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    75→
    76→## Workflow
    77→
    78→**Before committing, run verification:**
    79→```bash
    80→tspec test -p tspec
    81→tspec clippy
    82→tspec fmt --check
    83→```
    84→
    85→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
    86→```
    87→Committed abc123.
    88→
    89→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
    90→```
    91→
    92→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
    93→
    94→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
    95→
    96→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
    97→
    98→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
    99→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   100→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   101→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   102→
   103→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   104→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

The `CLAUDE.md` file is already loaded in context. Here's a summary:

**tspec** - A spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust on top of cargo, using TOML-based translation spec files.

Key points:
- **Architecture**: Commands implement an `Execute` trait, with simple passthroughs using `execute_cargo_subcommand()` and custom commands implementing their own logic
- **Modules**: `cmd/` for commands, `cli.rs` for CLI, `types.rs` for spec types, `tspec.rs` for spec loading, `cargo_build.rs` for build orchestration
- **Specs**: TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with `[cargo]`, `[rustc]`, and `[linker]` sections
- **Conventions**: Rust Edition 2024, conventional commits, POP naming for single-crate projects
- **Workflow**: Run `tspec test -p tspec`, `tspec clippy`, `tspec fmt --check` before committing
- **Git restrictions**: Claude cannot do checkout/merge/rebase due to `.claude/` session file circularity

Excellent! Now I have all the information I need to provide a comprehensive summary. Let me compile the findings.

## Research Summary: `cargo.target_dir` Spec Field Feature

### 1. Design Motivation & Original Problem

From **notes/chores-1.md** (lines 230-270), the `target_dir` feature addresses a critical issue:

**Problem**: When using different specs with the same target triple, builds overwrite each other:
```
tspec build -t tspec.static-opt.ts.toml  → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/
tspec build -t tspec.dyn-opt.ts.toml     → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/  (overwrites!)
```

This occurs because both specs generate binaries to the same output directory despite having different compilation parameters, making it impossible to compare builds side-by-side without manual directory management.

**Proposed Solution**: Add a `target_dir` field to the `[cargo]` section that supports template placeholder expansion to create separate output directories per spec.

### 2. Current Architecture

#### 2.1 Spec Types (`src/types.rs`)

The spec structure is organized in three main configuration sections:

```rust
pub struct Spec {
    pub panic: Option<PanicMode>,      // High-level panic mode
    pub strip: Option<StripMode>,      // High-level strip mode
    
    #[serde(default)]
    pub cargo: CargoConfig,            // Cargo build configuration
    #[serde(default)]
    pub rustc: RustcConfig,            // Rustc compilation config
    #[serde(default)]
    pub linker: LinkerConfig,          // Linker arguments
}

pub struct CargoConfig {
    pub profile: Option<Profile>,
    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
}
```

**Current Status**: `CargoConfig` does NOT have a `target_dir` field yet. This needs to be added.

#### 2.2 Spec Loading/Saving (`src/tspec.rs`)

- **Loading**: `load_spec()` reads and parses TOML files via `serde`
- **Serialization**: `serialize_spec()` converts specs to canonical TOML form (needed for hashing)
- **Hashing**: `hash_spec()` computes SHA256 and returns first 8 hex chars (e.g., `a1b2c3d4`)
- **Snapshots**: `save_spec_snapshot()` creates versioned snapshots like `{name}-001-a1b2c3d4.ts.toml`

These functions are simple pass-through to/from TOML and don't require modification for `target_dir` support (serde handles it automatically once the field is added).

#### 2.3 Build Orchestration (`src/cargo_build.rs`)

The `build_crate()` function:
1. Locates the project root and crate directory
2. Finds the tspec file (if one exists)
3. **Determines binary path** using `get_binary_path()` or `get_binary_path_simple()`
4. Generates temporary `build.rs` for linker flags if needed
5. Builds the cargo command with `apply_spec_to_command()`
6. Executes cargo

**Key observation**: The binary path is computed **before** cargo runs (lines 33-38). This is where `target_dir` substitution must happen.

#### 2.4 Binary Path Resolution (`src/find_paths.rs`)

The `get_binary_path()` function (lines 257-277) constructs paths like:

```rust
pub fn get_binary_path(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec, release: bool) -> PathBuf {
    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {
        spec.cargo.target_json.as_ref()
            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))
    });
    let profile_dir = if is_release { "release" } else { "debug" };
    
    match target {
        Some(t) => workspace
            .join("target")
            .join(t)
            .join(profile_dir)
            .join(crate_name),
        None => workspace.join("target").join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),
    }
}
```

**Current structure**: `target/{triple}/{profile}/{binary}`

**With target_dir**: Should become `target/{triple}/{target_dir}/{profile}/{binary}`

This is where the placeholder substitution (`<name>`, `<hash>`) must occur.

### 3. How Builds and Compares Currently Work

#### Build Flow (src/cmd/build.rs):
1. User runs `tspec build -t spec.toml`
2. `BuildCmd::execute()` calls `build_crate()`
3. `build_crate()` determines binary path via `get_binary_path()`
4. Cargo is invoked with `--target` flag (if target_triple/target_json specified)
5. Binary lands in the computed path
6. Return `BuildResult` with the binary path

#### Compare Flow (src/cmd/compare.rs → src/compare.rs):
1. User runs `tspec compare -p myapp -t spec1.toml -t spec2.toml`
2. For each spec:
   - Builds with `build_crate(crate_name, Some(spec_path))`
   - Optionally strips the binary
   - Measures size with `binary_size()`
3. Compares and displays results

**Critical issue with current design**: When two specs have the same target triple, both output to the same directory, causing overwrites. The compare operation must rebuild each spec sequentially, and the second build will overwrite the first binary before its size can be measured.

#### Test Discovery (src/all.rs, lines 175-194):
Test crates build and then discover test binaries by scanning the target directory:
```rust
let target_dir = workspace.root.join("target").join(profile);
let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
    .filter(|e| e.file_name().ends_with("-tests"))
    .collect();
```

**Impact of target_dir**: This scan would need to account for subdirectories if `target_dir` creates them. However, for test crates, the `.ts.toml` spec likely includes the target triple, so binaries would go to the same location regardless. The current logic doesn't need immediate changes, but it's worth noting.

### 4. Design Notes from chores-1.md

**Template Placeholder Support**:
```toml
[cargo]
target_dir = "my-builds/static"     # Explicit path (no substitution)
target_dir = "<name>"                # Spec filename sans .ts.toml (DEFAULT)
target_dir = "<hash>"                # Spec content hash (8-char)
target_dir = "<name>-<hash>"         # Combine both
target_dir = "builds/<hash>"         # Custom prefix + hash
target_dir = ""                       # Empty = no subdirectory (backwards compat)
```

**Default Behavior**:
- No `target_dir` specified → defaults to `<name>` (spec name without `.ts.toml`)
- Empty `target_dir = ""` → backward compatible, no subdirectory inserted

**Path Structure**: Insert between target triple and profile:
```
target/{triple}/{target_dir}/{profile}/{binary}
target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/static-opt/release/tspec
target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/a1b2c3d4/release/tspec
```

### 5. No Existing `--target-dir` or `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` Usage

Grep search found:
- `src/cargo_build.rs:232-234`: Uses `target_dir` locally for version script generation, not for build output
- `src/all.rs:177`: Uses `target_dir` locally for test binary discovery
- **No usage of `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` environment variable or `--target-dir` cargo flag**

This is important: tspec currently relies on cargo's default target directory placement and computes paths post-hoc. The design doesn't use `--target-dir` flag.

### 6. Implementation Requirements

To implement this feature, the following changes are needed:

**1. Type Changes (`src/types.rs`)**
- Add `target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig`
- Serde will automatically handle parsing/serialization

**2. Binary Path Resolution (`src/find_paths.rs`)**
- Modify `get_binary_path()` to:
  - Extract spec name from the tspec file path
  - Compute spec hash via `tspec::hash_spec()`
  - Substitute `<name>` and `<hash>` placeholders
  - Insert the resolved `target_dir` between triple and profile

**3. Build Orchestration (`src/cargo_build.rs`)**
- Pass spec filename/hash to `get_binary_path()` (currently only receives spec content)
- Option: Use `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` environment variable or `--target-dir` flag to ensure cargo writes to the correct location

**4. Snapshot/Compare Impact**
- `compare.rs` automatically benefits: each spec's binary goes to a different directory, enabling accurate size measurements
- `save_spec_snapshot()` in `tspec.rs` already extracts spec hash; no changes needed

### 7. Key Integration Points

1. **`build_crate()` signature change**: Currently takes only `tspec: Option<&str>` (path as string). Need to pass additional metadata (spec filename) to `get_binary_path()`.

2. **Path computation location**: `get_binary_path()` is called in two places:
   - `cargo_build.rs:35` - During build to compute expected output location
   - Tests in `find_paths.rs` - Needs placeholder expansion tests

3. **Backward compatibility**: Empty `target_dir = ""` maintains current behavior (no subdirectory), so existing specs without the field continue to work.

This design elegantly solves the overwrite problem while maintaining backward compatibility and enabling accurate spec comparison without complex workarounds.

Here's a summary of the research:

### The Problem
When two specs share the same target triple, builds overwrite each other:
```
tspec build -t static-opt.ts.toml  → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/
tspec build -t dyn-opt.ts.toml     → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/  (overwrites!)
```
This makes `tspec compare` unreliable.

### The Design (from chores-1.md)
Add `target_dir` to `[cargo]` with template placeholder expansion:
```toml
[cargo]
target_dir = "<name>"           # spec filename sans .ts.toml (proposed DEFAULT)
target_dir = "<hash>"           # 8-char spec content hash
target_dir = "<name>-<hash>"    # combined
target_dir = ""                 # empty = no subdir (backward compat)
```

Path structure inserts between triple and profile:
```
target/{triple}/{target_dir}/{profile}/{binary}
target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/static-opt/release/tspec
```

### What Needs Changing
1. **`types.rs`** - Add `target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig`
2. **`find_paths.rs`** - `get_binary_path()` needs placeholder substitution and to insert the resolved `target_dir` segment
3. **`cargo_build.rs`** - Pass `--target-dir` or `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` to cargo so it actually writes to the right place, plus pass spec metadata to path resolution
4. **Backward compat** - No `target_dir` field or `""` means no subdirectory (current behavior)

### Current State
- No `--target-dir` or `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` usage exists today
- tspec relies on cargo's default placement and computes paths post-hoc
- `compare` and `build_all` would automatically benefit once implemented

Now I have all four files. Let me create a comprehensive report analyzing the key aspects you asked for.

## Comprehensive Analysis Report

### 1. **src/types.rs** - Spec Type Definitions

**Key Structures:**
- `Spec` - Root struct with three main config sections
- `CargoConfig` - Flat struct with cargo-specific settings
- `RustcConfig` - Flat struct with rustc codegen settings
- `LinkerConfig` - Flat struct with linker arguments
- `Profile`, `OptLevel`, `PanicStrategy` - Enum types for spec options

**Important Note:** The `Spec` type does NOT contain any metadata (name, hash, file path). It's purely the configuration data. Name and hash must be threaded through separately.

---

### 2. **src/find_paths.rs** - Binary Path Resolution

**Key Functions:**

```rust
pub fn get_binary_path(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec, release: bool) -> PathBuf
```
- **Parameters:** workspace path, crate name, spec, release flag
- **Logic:** 
  - Determines `is_release` = `release || spec.cargo.profile == Some(Profile::Release)`
  - Extracts target from `spec.cargo.target_triple` OR filename stem from `spec.cargo.target_json`
  - Returns path: 
    - With target: `workspace/target/{target}/{profile}/{crate_name}`
    - Without target: `workspace/target/{profile}/{crate_name}`

```rust
pub fn get_binary_path_simple(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, release: bool) -> PathBuf
```
- **Parameters:** workspace, crate name, release flag (no spec)
- **Logic:** Returns `workspace/target/{profile}/{crate_name}`

**Critical Observation:** Both functions hardcode `target/` directory. There is NO support for:
- Custom `--target-dir` flag
- `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` environment variable
- Spec-aware target directory naming (e.g., `target-{spec_hash}/`)

---

### 3. **src/cargo_build.rs** - Build Orchestration

**Key Function:**

```rust
pub fn build_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult>
```
- **Parameters:** crate name, optional tspec path, release flag
- **Does NOT receive:** workspace, project_root, or spec directly
- **Internally:**
  1. Calls `find_project_root()` to locate workspace
  2. Calls `find_package_dir()` to locate crate
  3. Calls `find_tspec()` to locate spec file
  4. Calls `load_spec()` to parse spec
  5. Calls `get_binary_path()` with loaded spec
  6. Builds cargo command and applies spec via `apply_spec_to_command()`

**Version Script Handling (line 231-248):**
- Creates file at: `workspace/target/tspec-version.script` (hardcoded path)
- This is the ONLY place spec-specific paths are created, and it's hardcoded to `target/`

**Key Missing Data Flow:**
- `Spec` object is loaded but has no name/hash attached
- Binary path is computed from spec contents only
- No mechanism to track which spec was used for which build

**Target Directory Construction:**
- Only uses hardcoded `workspace/target/`
- Never passes `--target-dir` to cargo
- Never sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`

---

### 4. **src/tspec.rs** - Spec Loading and Hashing

**Key Functions:**

```rust
pub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec>
pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String>
```

**hash_spec Signature Details:**
```rust
pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String>
```
- Takes: Only the `Spec` object (not spec name, path, or any metadata)
- Returns: 8-character hex hash (first 4 bytes of SHA256)
- Process: Serializes spec to canonical TOML, hashes bytes

**Critical Limitation:** Function only receives `&Spec`, so caller cannot pass spec name. The name must be obtained separately and threaded through calling code.

**Snapshot Function:**

```rust
pub fn save_spec_snapshot(spec: &Spec, name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf>
```
- Creates file: `{name}-{seq:03}-{hash}{TSPEC_SUFFIX}`
- Example: `build-001-a1b2c3d4.ts.toml`
- Name and hash ARE available here but must be extracted from filename by caller

---

## Summary: Target Directory Construction & Spec Metadata Threading

| Aspect | Current State | Gap |
|--------|---------------|-----|
| **Binary Path Construction** | `workspace/target/{target?}/{profile}/{crate_name}` | No spec-aware naming |
| **Version Script Path** | `workspace/target/tspec-version.script` | Hardcoded, overwrites on each build |
| **--target-dir Support** | None | Never passed to cargo |
| **CARGO_TARGET_DIR Support** | None | Never set |
| **Spec Name in Types** | Not in `Spec` struct | Must thread separately |
| **Spec Hash in Types** | Not in `Spec` struct | Must compute on demand |
| **Spec Metadata Flow** | Path → load → compute hash, but hash not stored | Hash discarded after binary path calculation |

---

## Recommendations for Threading Spec Metadata

To enable spec-aware target directory naming, you would need:

1. **Extend `Spec` struct** OR create `SpecContext` wrapper:
   ```rust
   struct SpecContext {
       spec: Spec,
       name: String,
       hash: String,
   }
   ```

2. **Update `get_binary_path()` signature** to accept spec metadata:
   ```rust
   pub fn get_binary_path(
       workspace: &Path, 
       crate_name: &str, 
       spec: &Spec,
       spec_hash: &str,  // NEW
       release: bool
   ) -> PathBuf
   ```

3. **Construct spec-aware paths:**
   ```
   workspace/target-{hash}/{target?}/{profile}/{crate_name}
   ```

4. **Pass `--target-dir`** in `apply_spec_to_command()`:
   ```rust
   if let Some(hash) = spec_hash {
       let target_dir = workspace.join(format!("target-{}", hash));
       cmd.arg("--target-dir").arg(target_dir);
   }
   ```

All absolute file paths mentioned in this analysis are already shown in the code above.

Perfect! I found the section about per-spec target directories. Here's the complete content from `notes/chores-1.md`:

## Per-Spec Target Directories Section (chores-1.md, lines 230-270)

**Problem:** When using different specs with the same target triple, builds overwrite each other:
```
tspec build -t tspec.static-opt.ts.toml  → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/
tspec build -t tspec.dyn-opt.ts.toml     → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/  (overwrites!)
```

**Solution:** Add `target_dir` field to `[cargo]` section with template placeholder support:

```toml
[cargo]
# Explicit path (no substitution)
target_dir = "my-builds/static"

# Template with placeholders
target_dir = "<name>"           # spec name sans .ts.toml (DEFAULT)
target_dir = "<hash>"           # spec content hash
target_dir = "<name>-<hash>"    # combine both
target_dir = "builds/<hash>"    # custom prefix + hash
```

**Path structure:** Insert between target triple and profile:
```
target/{triple}/{target_dir}/{profile}/{binary}
target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/static-opt/release/tspec
target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/a1b2c3d4/release/tspec
```

**Behavior:**
- No `target_dir` specified → defaults to `<name>` (spec name sans .ts.toml)
- Empty `target_dir = ""` → no subdirectory (current behavior, backwards compat)
- Placeholders `<name>` and `<hash>` expanded at build time

**Implementation:**
1. Add `target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig` in types.rs
2. Update `get_binary_path()` in find_paths.rs to incorporate target_dir
3. Update cargo_build.rs to pass appropriate `--target-dir` or use CARGO_TARGET_DIR
4. Update compare.rs if needed

**Status:** Todo

---

## Related Discussions

**chores-2.md** - No direct discussion of per-spec target directories. That file focuses on the `Ts` command Execute trait conversion task (20260206).

**tspec-design.md** - No discussion of per-spec target directories. That file contains only the "20260202 - Augment vs Replace Cargo" design decision about whether tspec should augment or replace cargo.

The per-spec target directories feature is unique to the chores-1.md document and appears to be a planned feature to resolve build artifact conflicts when multiple specs use the same target triple.

Excellent! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the codebase. Let me compile my findings into a detailed report.

## Exploration Report: Binary Path Resolution and target_dir Impact in tspec

Based on a thorough exploration of the tspec codebase, here's how the compare, test, run, and build-all operations work and how they resolve binary paths:

---

### **1. Build Command (`src/cmd/build.rs`)**

**Call Flow:**
- Calls `build_crate(crate_name, tspec, release)` (lines 58, 48)
- Returns `BuildResult` with `binary_path`
- Optionally strips the binary if `--strip` flag is set (line 60)

**Binary Path Resolution:**
- Done in `src/cargo_build.rs` `build_crate()` (lines 20-81)
- For specs: calls `get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, &spec, release)` (line 35)
- Without specs: calls `get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)` (line 37)
- **Critical**: Binary path is calculated BEFORE build happens and based on workspace root + target directory + profile (debug/release) + optional target triple/JSON

---

### **2. Run Command (`src/cmd/run.rs`)**

**Call Flow:**
- Single package mode: builds with `build_crate()` (line 54), then runs with `run_binary(&result.binary_path, &args)` (line 58)
- All packages mode: calls `run_all()` from `src/all.rs`

**Binary Path Consumption:**
- `run_binary()` in `src/run.rs` takes the calculated path and executes it directly (line 8)
- **Assumption**: The binary exists at the calculated path after build completes

---

### **3. Test Command (`src/cmd/test.rs`)**

**Call Flow:**
- Single package mode: calls `test_crate(&name, tspec, release)` (line 53)
- All packages mode: calls `test_all()` from `src/all.rs`

**Implementation Detail:**
- `test_crate()` in `src/testing.rs` (lines 21-73) does NOT resolve a binary path
- It invokes `cargo test` directly with the spec applied
- **No hardcoded binary path assumptions** - cargo handles this internally

---

### **4. Compare Command (`src/cmd/compare.rs`)**

**Call Flow:**
- Calls `compare_specs()` in `src/compare.rs` for each spec file (line 31)
- For each spec: calls `build_crate(crate_name, spec_path, release)` (line 59)
- Gets binary size with `binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)` (line 67)
- Optionally strips before measuring (line 63)

**Binary Path Consumption:**
- Uses the returned `binary_path` from `build_crate()` to measure file size
- **Critical dependency**: Needs accurate binary path to locate the built artifact

---

### **5. Batch Operations (`src/all.rs`)**

**Build All (lines 23-69):**
- Iterates workspace members, calls `build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release)` (line 36)
- Gets size with `binary_size(&build_result.binary_path)` (line 44)
- Optionally strips (line 40)

**Run All (lines 71-115):**
- Calls `build_crate()` (line 83)
- Runs binary with `run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[])` (line 88)

**Test All (lines 117-253):**
- Regular crates: calls `test_crate()` (line 134) - no binary path needed
- Test crates (special case, lines 157-250):
  - **Hardcoded assumption**: Assumes test binaries are in `workspace.root/target/{profile}` (lines 176-177)
  - Reads directory directly to find `-tests` binaries (lines 180-194)
  - **Critical hardcoding**: Line 177 does `workspace.root.join("target").join(profile)`
  - This will **break if target_dir changes** because it's hardcoded to look in root/target only

---

### **6. Install Command (`src/cmd/install.rs`)**

**Note:** Uses `cargo install --path`, not custom binary path resolution
- No custom path calculations
- No target_dir dependencies

---

### **Key Binary Path Resolution Functions (`src/find_paths.rs`)**

**`get_binary_path()` (lines 257-277):**
```
workspace/target/[target_triple/]{debug|release}/[crate_name]
```
- Determines if release from spec or flag (line 258)
- Extracts target triple/JSON from spec (lines 260-265)
- Constructs path with hardcoded "target" directory (lines 270-275)

**`get_binary_path_simple()` (lines 280-283):**
```
workspace/target/{debug|release}/[crate_name]
```
- Also hardcoded "target" directory

---

### **Critical Hardcoded Assumptions About target_dir:**

1. **Line 270-275 in `find_paths.rs`**: `workspace.join("target")`
   - Consumers: `build_crate()`, `compare_specs()`, `run_binary()`

2. **Line 275 in `find_paths.rs`**: `workspace.join("target")`
   - Consumers: `build_crate()`, `compare_specs()`, `run_binary()`

3. **Line 282 in `find_paths.rs`**: `workspace.join("target")`
   - Consumers: Simple builds (no spec)

4. **Line 177 in `all.rs`**: `workspace.root.join("target").join(profile)`
   - **MOST CRITICAL**: Test harness for test crates looks directly in target directory
   - This is the **most dangerous hardcoding** because it scans the filesystem

5. **Line 232 in `cargo_build.rs`**: `workspace.join("target")`
   - Used for version script generation (not binary path, but target_dir related)

---

### **Summary of Affected Consumers:**

| Operation | Binary Path Used | Impact | Severity |
|-----------|------------------|--------|----------|
| Build | Yes - returned from `build_crate()` | Output reporting only | Low |
| Run | Yes - executed | Binary must be found to run | High |
| Test (regular) | No - cargo internal | Cargo handles target_dir | None |
| Test (test crates) | Yes - filesystem scan in target/{profile} | Filesystem scan fails | Critical |
| Compare | Yes - read for size | Size measurement fails | High |
| Install | No - cargo internal | N/A | None |
| Build All | Yes - returned from `build_crate()` | Sizing fails | High |
| Run All | Yes - returned from `build_crate()` | Execution fails | High |

---

### **Conclusion:**

**Any change to target_dir would require updates in:**

1. **`src/find_paths.rs`** - Both `get_binary_path()` and `get_binary_path_simple()` must be parameterized
2. **`src/all.rs`** - Line 177 test crate scanning hardcodes `workspace.root.join("target")`
3. **`src/cargo_build.rs`** - Line 232 version script generation (though this is less critical)

The **most vulnerable point** is the test crate harness in `all.rs` which does a filesystem scan of the hardcoded target directory.

Now I have a thorough understanding of every relevant file. Here is the detailed implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan: `cargo.target_dir` Spec Field

### 1. Overview and Design

The goal is to add a `target_dir` field to `CargoConfig` that allows per-spec target directory isolation. When two specs share the same target triple, their builds currently overwrite each other. With `target_dir`, the path structure becomes:

```
target/{target_dir}/{triple}/{profile}/{binary}
```

The field supports template placeholders:
- `<name>` -- the spec filename without the `.ts.toml` suffix
- `<hash>` -- first 8 hex chars of the spec's SHA-256 content hash

When `target_dir` is `None` or `Some("")`, behavior is unchanged (backward compatible).

### 2. Critical Design Decision: Hash Circularity

The `hash_spec()` function in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs` works by serializing the `Spec` to TOML and hashing the result. If `target_dir` contains the literal string `"<hash>"`, that literal is what gets serialized and hashed. The hash is then used to **expand** the placeholder at build time. There is no circularity because:

- The `Spec` struct stores the raw template string (e.g., `"<name>"` or `"<name>-<hash>"`)
- `hash_spec()` hashes the template string as-is
- Template expansion happens separately, at build time, producing a runtime value never stored back in the Spec

This is the correct design -- the hash is of the spec content (including the literal `<hash>` template text), not the expanded value.

### 3. Step-by-Step Implementation

#### Step 1: Add `target_dir` to `CargoConfig` (in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/types.rs`)

Add a new field to `CargoConfig`:

```rust
pub struct CargoConfig {
    pub profile: Option<Profile>,
    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    /// Custom target directory subdirectory.
    /// Supports <name> (spec filename sans .ts.toml) and <hash> (8-char content hash).
    /// When set, cargo receives --target-dir=target/{expanded_value}
    pub target_dir: Option<String>,
}
```

Because `CargoConfig` derives `Default` and uses `#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]`, the `Option<String>` field will default to `None` and be omitted from serialized TOML when `None`. No changes needed for serde behavior. No changes needed to `CargoConfig::default()` since it is auto-derived.

#### Step 2: Add the `expand_target_dir` helper function (new function in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs`)

This is the template expansion logic. Place it in `tspec.rs` alongside `hash_spec` since it needs the hash:

```rust
/// Expand template placeholders in target_dir.
/// - `<name>` is replaced with the spec filename sans `.ts.toml`
/// - `<hash>` is replaced with the 8-char content hash
/// Returns None if target_dir is None or empty after expansion.
pub fn expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    let raw = match &spec.cargo.target_dir {
        Some(td) if !td.is_empty() => td,
        _ => return Ok(None),
    };

    let mut expanded = raw.clone();

    if expanded.contains("<name>") {
        expanded = expanded.replace("<name>", spec_name);
    }

    if expanded.contains("<hash>") {
        let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
        expanded = expanded.replace("<hash>", &hash);
    }

    if expanded.is_empty() {
        Ok(None)
    } else {
        Ok(Some(expanded))
    }
}
```

The `spec_name` parameter is the spec filename with the `.ts.toml` suffix stripped. For example, for file `tspec.static-opt.ts.toml`, the name would be `tspec.static-opt`.

#### Step 3: Add `spec_name_from_path` helper (new function in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs`)

Extract the spec name from a file path. This utility is needed by multiple callers:

```rust
/// Extract spec name from path: strip the .ts.toml suffix (or .toml fallback)
pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {
    let filename = path
        .file_name()
        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_default();

    filename
        .strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(".toml"))
        .unwrap_or(&filename)
        .to_string()
}
```

#### Step 4: Modify `get_binary_path` in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/find_paths.rs`

The function currently has this signature:
```rust
pub fn get_binary_path(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec, release: bool) -> PathBuf
```

Add an `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter. The caller is responsible for computing it (to avoid duplicating spec name extraction logic). New signature:

```rust
pub fn get_binary_path(
    workspace: &Path,
    crate_name: &str,
    spec: &Spec,
    release: bool,
    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
) -> PathBuf
```

Implementation change -- the base path construction changes from:

```rust
// current
workspace.join("target")
```

to:

```rust
let base = match expanded_target_dir {
    Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
    None => workspace.join("target"),
};
```

Then the rest of the function uses `base` instead of `workspace.join("target")`:

```rust
match target {
    Some(t) => base.join(t).join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),
    None => base.join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),
}
```

**Note**: `get_binary_path_simple` does not need changes since it is for no-spec builds.

#### Step 5: Modify `build_crate` in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs`

This is the primary orchestration point. Currently at line 33-35:

```rust
let binary_path = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, &spec, release)
```

Change to compute expanded_target_dir and pass it through:

```rust
let binary_path = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    let spec_name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    let expanded_td = expand_target_dir(&spec, &spec_name)?;
    get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, &spec, release, expanded_td.as_deref())
```

Also in the cargo command section (line 41-57), pass `--target-dir` to the cargo command. This should be added inside the `apply_spec_to_command` call or alongside it. The cleanest approach is to add it in `apply_spec_to_command`. But `apply_spec_to_command` does not currently have access to the expanded target_dir. Two options:

**Option A**: Add `expanded_target_dir` parameter to `apply_spec_to_command`.
**Option B**: Add `--target-dir` directly in `build_crate` after calling `apply_spec_to_command`.

Option A is better because `apply_spec_to_command` is also called from `testing.rs`, and both need the same `--target-dir` logic. New signature:

```rust
pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
    cmd: &mut Command,
    spec: &Spec,
    workspace: &Path,
    release: bool,
    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
) -> Result<()>
```

Inside `apply_spec_to_command`, add near the beginning (after the panic_mode handling, before cargo config):

```rust
if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
    let target_dir = workspace.join("target").join(td);
    cmd.arg("--target-dir").arg(&target_dir);
}
```

**Important note about --target-dir semantics**: When cargo receives `--target-dir target/foo`, it outputs to `target/foo/{triple}/{profile}/`. This matches the desired path structure `target/{target_dir}/{triple}/{profile}/{binary}`. No additional nesting occurs.

The second `load_spec` call at line 42 is redundant (the spec is loaded twice -- once for binary path, once for command). This is an existing inefficiency. For this change, compute `expanded_td` once and reuse it. Refactored flow:

```rust
let (binary_path, spec_for_build, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    let spec_name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    let expanded_td = expand_target_dir(&spec, &spec_name)?;
    let binary_path = get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, &spec, release, expanded_td.as_deref());
    (binary_path, Some(spec), expanded_td)
} else {
    (get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release), None, None)
};
```

Then later:

```rust
let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec_for_build {
    // ... existing logic but use spec instead of loading again
    apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;
    // ...
```

This eliminates the double `load_spec` call as a side benefit.

#### Step 6: Modify `test_crate` in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/testing.rs`

The `test_crate` function also calls `apply_spec_to_command`. Add the same expanded_target_dir logic:

```rust
let status = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    let spec_name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    let expanded_td = expand_target_dir(&spec, &spec_name)?;
    // ... existing setup ...
    apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;
    cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?
} else {
    // ... no change for no-spec path ...
};
```

Add the necessary imports at the top of `testing.rs`:
```rust
use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
```

#### Step 7: Fix `test_all` in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs`

Lines 176-177 currently hardcode the target directory for scanning test binaries:

```rust
let profile = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
let target_dir = workspace.root.join("target").join(profile);
```

This is problematic because when `target_dir` is set, the test binaries will be in `target/{expanded_td}/{profile}/` instead. However, the `test_all` function calls `build_crate` which returns a `BuildResult` containing the binary path. The test binary scanning logic at line 180 scans the target directory for executables ending in `-tests`.

The fix requires `build_crate` to also return the effective target directory (or the expanded_target_dir value) so that `test_all` can construct the correct scan path. Modify `BuildResult`:

```rust
pub struct BuildResult {
    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
    /// The effective target-dir base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
    pub target_base: PathBuf,
}
```

In `build_crate`, set `target_base`:
```rust
let target_base = match &expanded_td {
    Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
    None => workspace.join("target"),
};
// ...
Ok(BuildResult { binary_path, target_base })
```

Then in `test_all` (line 176-177), use the build result to determine the scan directory:

```rust
// After build_crate succeeds (line 162), capture the target_base
if let Ok(build_result) = build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
    // ... existing code ...
    let profile = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
    let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile);
    // ... rest of scanning logic unchanged ...
}
```

Wait -- looking more carefully, the `build_crate` result at line 162 is checked with `if let Err(e)`, discarding the Ok value. The code then constructs `target_dir` independently. Need to refactor to:

```rust
let build_result = match build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
    Ok(r) => r,
    Err(e) => {
        results.push(OpResult { ... });
        if fail_fast { return results; }
        continue;
    }
};

let profile = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile);
```

#### Step 8: Add `cargo.target_dir` to `tspec ts set` in `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/ts_cmd/set.rs`

Add a new match arm in `apply_value` (around line 75, after `cargo.target_triple`):

```rust
"cargo.target_dir" => {
    spec.cargo.target_dir = Some(value.to_string());
}
```

#### Step 9: Update version script path in `apply_spec_to_command`

In `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs` lines 232-234, the version script is written to `workspace.join("target")`:

```rust
let target_dir = workspace.join("target");
let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&target_dir);
let version_script_path = target_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
```

When `target_dir` is set, this should use the expanded target directory to avoid collisions between specs. Change to:

```rust
let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
    Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
    None => workspace.join("target"),
};
let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
```

This requires `expanded_target_dir` to be in scope, which it will be since we added it as a parameter.

### 4. Files to Modify (Summary)

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `src/types.rs` | Add `target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig` |
| `src/tspec.rs` | Add `expand_target_dir()` and `spec_name_from_path()` helpers |
| `src/find_paths.rs` | Add `expanded_target_dir` param to `get_binary_path()` |
| `src/cargo_build.rs` | Compute expanded target_dir, pass to `get_binary_path` and `apply_spec_to_command`, add `--target-dir` flag, add `target_base` to `BuildResult`, fix version script path |
| `src/testing.rs` | Compute expanded target_dir, pass to `apply_spec_to_command` |
| `src/all.rs` | Use `build_result.target_base` for test binary scanning |
| `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` | Add `cargo.target_dir` match arm |

### 5. Function Signature Changes (Complete List)

1. `get_binary_path(workspace, crate_name, spec, release)` becomes `get_binary_path(workspace, crate_name, spec, release, expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>)`

2. `apply_spec_to_command(cmd, spec, workspace, release)` becomes `apply_spec_to_command(cmd, spec, workspace, release, expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>)`

3. `BuildResult` gains field: `target_base: PathBuf`

### 6. New Functions

1. `pub fn expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>>` in `src/tspec.rs`
2. `pub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String` in `src/tspec.rs`

### 7. Test Strategy

**Unit tests for `expand_target_dir` (add to `src/tspec.rs` tests module):**

```rust
#[test]
fn expand_target_dir_none() {
    let spec = Spec::default();
    assert_eq!(expand_target_dir(&spec, "foo").unwrap(), None);
}

#[test]
fn expand_target_dir_empty() {
    let mut spec = Spec::default();
    spec.cargo.target_dir = Some("".to_string());
    assert_eq!(expand_target_dir(&spec, "foo").unwrap(), None);
}

#[test]
fn expand_target_dir_literal() {
    let mut spec = Spec::default();
    spec.cargo.target_dir = Some("my-subdir".to_string());
    assert_eq!(expand_target_dir(&spec, "foo").unwrap(), Some("my-subdir".to_string()));
}

#[test]
fn expand_target_dir_name_placeholder() {
    let mut spec = Spec::default();
    spec.cargo.target_dir = Some("<name>".to_string());
    assert_eq!(expand_target_dir(&spec, "static-opt").unwrap(), Some("static-opt".to_string()));
}

#[test]
fn expand_target_dir_hash_placeholder() {
    let mut spec = Spec::default();
    spec.cargo.target_dir = Some("<hash>".to_string());
    let result = expand_target_dir(&spec, "foo").unwrap().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(result.len(), 8);  // 8-char hex hash
    assert!(result.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()));
}

#[test]
fn expand_target_dir_name_and_hash() {
    let mut spec = Spec::default();
    spec.cargo.target_dir = Some("<name>-<hash>".to_string());
    let result = expand_target_dir(&spec, "opt").unwrap().unwrap();
    assert!(result.starts_with("opt-"));
    assert_eq!(result.len(), 4 + 8); // "opt-" + 8-char hash
}
```

**Unit tests for `spec_name_from_path` (add to `src/tspec.rs` tests module):**

```rust
#[test]
fn spec_name_from_ts_toml() {
    let path = PathBuf::from("/foo/bar/tspec.static-opt.ts.toml");
    assert_eq!(spec_name_from_path(&path), "tspec.static-opt");
}

#[test]
fn spec_name_from_plain_toml() {
    let path = PathBuf::from("/foo/bar/minimal.toml");
    assert_eq!(spec_name_from_path(&path), "minimal");
}

#[test]
fn spec_name_no_known_suffix() {
    let path = PathBuf::from("/foo/bar/weird.txt");
    assert_eq!(spec_name_from_path(&path), "weird.txt");
}
```

**Unit tests for `get_binary_path` with target_dir (add to `src/find_paths.rs` tests module):**

```rust
#[test]
fn get_binary_path_with_target_dir() {
    let workspace = Path::new("/workspace");
    let spec = Spec {
        cargo: CargoConfig {
            target_triple: Some("x86_64-unknown-linux-musl".to_string()),
            ..Default::default()
        },
        ..Default::default()
    };
    let path = get_binary_path(workspace, "myapp", &spec, true, Some("static-opt"));
    assert_eq!(
        path,
        PathBuf::from("/workspace/target/static-opt/x86_64-unknown-linux-musl/release/myapp")
    );
}

#[test]
fn get_binary_path_target_dir_none() {
    let workspace = Path::new("/workspace");
    let spec = Spec {
        cargo: CargoConfig {
            target_triple: Some("x86_64-unknown-linux-musl".to_string()),
            ..Default::default()
        },
        ..Default::default()
    };
    // None = backward compatible
    let path = get_binary_path(workspace, "myapp", &spec, true, None);
    assert_eq!(
        path,
        PathBuf::from("/workspace/target/x86_64-unknown-linux-musl/release/myapp")
    );
}

#[test]
fn get_binary_path_target_dir_no_triple() {
    let workspace = Path::new("/workspace");
    let spec = Spec::default();
    let path = get_binary_path(workspace, "myapp", &spec, false, Some("custom"));
    assert_eq!(
        path,
        PathBuf::from("/workspace/target/custom/debug/myapp")
    );
}

**Update existing `get_binary_path` tests:** All existing tests in `find_paths.rs` that call `get_binary_path` need the new 5th argument `None` appended (backward-compat case). There are 7 such tests.

### 8. Implementation Order

1. **`src/types.rs`** -- Add the field. This is the foundation; everything depends on it.
2. **`src/tspec.rs`** -- Add `spec_name_from_path` and `expand_target_dir`. These are pure functions with no downstream dependencies, testable in isolation.
3. **`src/find_paths.rs`** -- Add parameter to `get_binary_path`. Update all existing call sites to pass `None` to keep tests green.
4. **`src/cargo_build.rs`** -- Add parameter to `apply_spec_to_command`. Refactor `build_crate` to compute expanded target_dir, eliminate double `load_spec`, add `target_base` to `BuildResult`, pass `--target-dir` to cargo, fix version script path.
5. **`src/testing.rs`** -- Thread expanded_target_dir through to `apply_spec_to_command`.
6. **`src/all.rs`** -- Use `build_result.target_base` for test binary scanning.
7. **`src/ts_cmd/set.rs`** -- Add `cargo.target_dir` key.
8. **Test data + integration tests** -- Add `tests/data/with-target-dir.ts.toml`, update `tests/tspec_test.rs`.
9. **Run verification** -- `tspec test -p tspec`, `tspec clippy`, `tspec fmt --check`.

### 9. Potential Challenges

**A. Existing tests break from signature change.** The `get_binary_path` and `apply_spec_to_command` signature changes will break all callers. Fix is mechanical -- pass `None` everywhere for backward-compat. There are 7 test call sites for `get_binary_path` and 2 call sites for `apply_spec_to_command`.

**B. `all.rs` test binary scanning.** The `test_all` function currently drops the `build_crate` result for test members and reconstructs the target path. After this change, it must use the `BuildResult.target_base`. The refactoring at step 7 handles this.

**C. `compare.rs` flow.** The `compare_specs` function calls `build_crate` for each spec. Since `build_crate` internally handles `--target-dir`, each spec gets its own target directory automatically. The returned `BuildResult.binary_path` already points to the correct location. No changes needed to `compare.rs` itself.

**D. Version script collision.** Two specs building concurrently (or sequentially overwriting) could collide on `target/tspec-version.script`. Step 9 of the plan fixes this by placing the version script in the expanded target directory.

**E. Clean command.** The `CleanCmd` passes through to `cargo clean`, which cleans the default `target/` directory. With `target_dir` subdirectories, `cargo clean` will still work because the subdirectories are inside `target/`. No special handling needed.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/types.rs` - Add `target_dir` field to `CargoConfig` struct
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs` - Add `expand_target_dir` and `spec_name_from_path` helper functions
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs` - Core orchestration: thread target_dir through build, add `--target-dir` flag, update `BuildResult`
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/find_paths.rs` - Update `get_binary_path` to incorporate expanded target_dir in path construction
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs` - Fix test binary scanning to use `BuildResult.target_base` instead of hardcoded path

    18→
    19→/// Build a crate with a spec, returns the binary path on success
    20→pub fn build_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult> {
    21→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    22→    let crate_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, crate_name)?;
    23→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&crate_dir, tspec)?;
    24→
    25→    // Get actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when crate_name is a path)
    26→    let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&crate_dir)?;
    27→
    28→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    29→    let build_rs_path = crate_dir.join("build.rs");
    30→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    31→
    32→    // Determine binary path based on spec
    33→    let binary_path = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    34→        let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    35→        get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, &spec, release)
    36→    } else {
    37→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)
    38→    };
    39→
    40→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build
    41→    let status = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    42→        let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    43→        println!("Building {} with spec {}", pkg_name, path.display());
    44→
    45→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
    46→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    47→        if has_linker_args && !had_build_rs {
    48→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, &spec)?;
    49→        }
    50→
    51→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(&spec)?;
    52→        cmd.arg("build");
    53→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
    54→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    55→
    56→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, release)?;
    57→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
    58→    } else {
    59→        println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
    60→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    61→        cmd.arg("build");
    62→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
    63→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    64→        if release {
    65→            cmd.arg("--release");
    66→        }
    67→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
    68→    };
    69→
    70→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
    71→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
    72→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    if !status.success() {
    76→        bail!("cargo build failed");
    77→    }
    78→
    79→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
    80→    Ok(BuildResult { binary_path })
    81→}

    83→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
    84→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
    85→    let mut lines = vec![
    86→        "// Generated by tspec - do not edit".to_string(),
    87→        "fn main() {".to_string(),
    88→    ];
    89→
    90→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
    91→        lines.push(format!(
    92→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
    93→            crate_name, arg
    94→        ));
    95→    }
    96→
    97→    lines.push("}".to_string());
    98→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
    99→    Ok(())
   100→}
   101→
   102→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
   103→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   104→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
   105→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
   106→
   107→    // BuildStd requires nightly
   108→    let has_build_std = !spec.rustc.build_std.is_empty();
   109→
   110→    // Unstable cargo flags require nightly
   111→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   112→
   113→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   114→}
   115→
   116→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   117→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec) -> Result<Command> {
   118→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   119→
   120→    if requires_nightly(spec) {
   121→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   122→    }
   123→
   124→    Ok(cmd)
   125→}
   126→
   127→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command
   128→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   129→    cmd: &mut Command,
   130→    spec: &Spec,
   131→    workspace: &Path,
   132→    release: bool,
   133→) -> Result<()> {
   134→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   135→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   136→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   137→    {
   138→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   139→    }
   140→
   141→    // Handle cargo config
   142→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   143→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   144→        match profile {
   145→            Profile::Release => {
   146→                cmd.arg("--release");
   147→            }
   148→            Profile::Debug => {
   149→                // Debug is default, no flag needed
   150→            }
   151→        }
   152→    }
   153→
   154→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   155→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   156→    }
   157→
   158→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   159→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   163→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   164→    }
   165→
   166→    // If no profile in spec but release flag passed, use release
   167→    if !has_profile && release {
   168→        cmd.arg("--release");
   169→    }
   170→
   171→    // Collect rustc flags
   172→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   173→
   174→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   175→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   176→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   177→    {
   178→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   179→    }
   180→
   181→    // Handle rustc config
   182→    let rustc = &spec.rustc;
   183→
   184→    if let Some(level) = &rustc.opt_level {
   185→        let lvl = match level {
   186→            OptLevel::O0 => "0",
   187→            OptLevel::O1 => "1",
   188→            OptLevel::O2 => "2",
   189→            OptLevel::O3 => "3",
   190→            OptLevel::Os => "s",
   191→            OptLevel::Oz => "z",
   192→        };
   193→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C opt-level={}", lvl));
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    if let Some(strategy) = &rustc.panic {
   197→        let s = match strategy {
   198→            PanicStrategy::Abort => "abort",
   199→            PanicStrategy::Unwind => "unwind",
   200→            PanicStrategy::ImmediateAbort => "immediate-abort",
   201→        };
   202→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", s));
   203→    }
   204→
   205→    if let Some(true) = rustc.lto {
   206→        rustc_flags.push("-C lto=true".to_string());
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    if let Some(n) = rustc.codegen_units {
   210→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C codegen-units={}", n));
   211→    }
   212→
   213→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   214→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   215→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   216→    {
   217→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   218→    }
   219→
   220→    if !rustc.build_std.is_empty() {
   221→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   222→        let crates_str = rustc.build_std.join(",");
   223→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   224→    }
   225→
   226→    for flag in &rustc.flags {
   227→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   228→    }
   229→
   230→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   231→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   232→        let target_dir = workspace.join("target");
   233→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&target_dir);
   234→        let version_script_path = target_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   235→
   236→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   237→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   238→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   239→
   240→        let mut f =
   241→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   242→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   243→
   244→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   245→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   246→            version_script_path.display()
   247→        ));
   248→    }
   249→
   250→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   251→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   252→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   253→    }
   254→
   255→    Ok(())
   256→}

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use glob::Pattern;
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::{Profile, Spec};
     7→
     8→/// Check if TOML content has an exact section header like `[workspace]` or `[package]`
     9→/// Only matches lines where the trimmed content equals `[section]` exactly
    10→fn has_toml_section_exact(content: &str, section: &str) -> bool {
    11→    let header = format!("[{}]", section);
    12→    content.lines().any(|line| line.trim() == header)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Extract crate name from Cargo.toml
    16→pub fn get_crate_name(crate_dir: &Path) -> Result<String> {
    17→    let cargo_toml = crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    18→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)
    19→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml.display()))?;
    20→
    21→    // Simple parsing - look for name = "..." in [package] section
    22→    let mut in_package = false;
    23→    for line in content.lines() {
    24→        let line = line.trim();
    25→        if line == "[package]" {
    26→            in_package = true;
    27→            continue;
    28→        }
    29→        if line.starts_with('[') {
    30→            in_package = false;
    31→            continue;
    32→        }
    33→        if in_package
    34→            && line.starts_with("name")
    35→            && let Some(eq_pos) = line.find('=')
    36→        {
    37→            let value = line[eq_pos + 1..].trim();
    38→            let value = value.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
    39→            return Ok(value.to_string());
    40→        }
    41→    }
    42→    bail!("could not find package name in {}", cargo_toml.display());
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Find the project root by looking for Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package]
    46→/// For workspaces, returns the directory containing the workspace Cargo.toml
    47→/// For POPs (Plain Old Packages), returns the directory containing the package Cargo.toml
    48→pub fn find_project_root() -> Result<PathBuf> {
    49→    let mut dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
    50→    let mut package_root: Option<PathBuf> = None;
    51→
    52→    loop {
    53→        let cargo_toml = dir.join("Cargo.toml");
    54→        if cargo_toml.exists() {
    55→            let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml)?;
    56→            // Workspace takes precedence
    57→            if has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace") {
    58→                return Ok(dir);
    59→            }
    60→            // Remember the first (deepest) package we find as potential POP root
    61→            if package_root.is_none() && has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package") {
    62→                package_root = Some(dir.clone());
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        if !dir.pop() {
    66→            // No workspace found, use the POP root if we found one
    67→            if let Some(root) = package_root {
    68→                return Ok(root);
    69→            }
    70→            bail!("could not find project root (no Cargo.toml with [workspace] or [package])");
    71→        }
    72→    }
    73→}
    74→
    75→/// Check if a project root is a POP (Plain Old Package) vs a workspace
    76→pub fn is_pop(project_root: &Path) -> bool {
    77→    let cargo_toml = project_root.join("Cargo.toml");
    78→    if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml) {
    79→        // POP has [package] but no [workspace]
    80→        has_toml_section_exact(&content, "package")
    81→            && !has_toml_section_exact(&content, "workspace")
    82→    } else {
    83→        false
    84→    }
    85→}
    86→
    87→/// Resolve package directory from optional name, defaulting to current directory
    88→/// If package is None, uses current directory (must contain Cargo.toml)
    89→/// If package is Some, looks up the package by name
    90→pub fn resolve_package_dir(workspace: &Path, package: Option<&str>) -> Result<PathBuf> {
    91→    match package {
    92→        Some(name) => find_package_dir(workspace, name),
    93→        None => {
    94→            let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
    95→            if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
    96→                Ok(cwd)
    97→            } else {
    98→                bail!(
    99→                    "not in a package directory (no Cargo.toml found). Use -p to specify a package."
   100→                )
   101→            }
   102→        }
   103→    }
   104→}
   105→
   106→/// Find a package's directory - tries as path first, then searches standard locations
   107→/// For POPs, checks if name matches the root package
   108→pub fn find_package_dir(project_root: &Path, name: &str) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   109→    // Try as path first (relative or absolute)
   110→    let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   111→    if as_path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   112→        return Ok(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   113→    }
   114→
   115→    // For POPs, check if name matches the root package
   116→    if is_pop(project_root) {
   117→        if let Ok(pkg_name) = get_crate_name(project_root)
   118→            && pkg_name == name
   119→        {
   120→            return Ok(project_root.to_path_buf());
   121→        }
   122→        bail!(
   123→            "package '{}' not found (this is a single-package project with package '{}')",
   124→            name,
   125→            get_crate_name(project_root).unwrap_or_else(|_| "unknown".to_string())
   126→        );
   127→    }
   128→
   129→    // Workspace: search libs/, apps/, tools/
   130→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps", "tools"] {
   131→        let path = project_root.join(prefix).join(name);
   132→        if path.join("Cargo.toml").exists() {
   133→            return Ok(path);
   134→        }
   135→    }
   136→
   137→    // Special case: nested test crates like libs/rlibc-x2/tests
   138→    for prefix in ["libs", "apps"] {
   139→        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(project_root.join(prefix))
   140→            .into_iter()
   141→            .flatten()
   142→            .flatten()
   143→        {
   144→            let nested = entry.path().join("tests");
   145→            if nested.join("Cargo.toml").exists()
   146→                && let Ok(pkg_name) = get_crate_name(&nested)
   147→                && pkg_name == name
   148→            {
   149→                return Ok(nested);
   150→            }
   151→        }
   152→    }
   153→
   154→    bail!("package '{}' not found", name);
   155→}
   156→
   157→/// Find the tspec for a crate - tries as path first, then relative to crate_dir
   158→/// Returns None if no tspec exists (plain cargo build will be used)
   159→pub fn find_tspec(crate_dir: &Path, explicit: Option<&str>) -> Result<Option<PathBuf>> {
   160→    match explicit {
   161→        Some(name) => {
   162→            // Try as path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   163→            let as_path = PathBuf::from(name);
   164→            if as_path.exists() {
   165→                return Ok(Some(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path)));
   166→            }
   167→
   168→            // Fallback: relative to crate directory
   169→            let in_crate = crate_dir.join(name);
   170→            if in_crate.exists() {
   171→                return Ok(Some(in_crate));
   172→            }
   173→
   174→            // Try with TSPEC_SUFFIX if name has no extension
   175→            if !name.contains('.') {
   176→                let with_suffix = crate_dir.join(format!("{}{}", name, TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   177→                if with_suffix.exists() {
   178→                    return Ok(Some(with_suffix));
   179→                }
   180→            }
   181→
   182→            bail!("tspec not found: {}", name);
   183→        }
   184→        None => {
   185→            let default = crate_dir.join(format!("tspec{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX));
   186→            if default.exists() {
   187→                Ok(Some(default))
   188→            } else {
   189→                Ok(None) // No tspec = plain cargo build
   190→            }
   191→        }
   192→    }
   193→}
   194→
   195→/// Find multiple tspecs by glob patterns
   196→/// If no patterns given, defaults to "tspec*{TSPEC_SUFFIX}"
   197→/// Returns sorted list of paths, errors if none found
   198→pub fn find_tspecs(crate_dir: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>> {
   199→    let default_pattern = format!("tspec*{}", TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   200→    let patterns: Vec<&str> = if patterns.is_empty() {
   201→        vec![&default_pattern]
   202→    } else {
   203→        patterns.iter().map(|s| s.as_str()).collect()
   204→    };
   205→
   206→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   207→
   208→    for pattern in &patterns {
   209→        // Try as literal path first (relative to cwd or absolute)
   210→        let as_path = PathBuf::from(pattern);
   211→        if as_path.exists() && as_path.is_file() {
   212→            results.push(as_path.canonicalize().unwrap_or(as_path));
   213→            continue;
   214→        }
   215→
   216→        // Try as literal path relative to crate_dir
   217→        let in_crate = crate_dir.join(pattern);
   218→        if in_crate.exists() && in_crate.is_file() {
   219→            results.push(in_crate);
   220→            continue;
   221→        }
   222→
   223→        // Try as glob pattern in crate_dir
   224→        let glob_pattern =
   225→            Pattern::new(pattern).with_context(|| format!("invalid glob pattern: {}", pattern))?;
   226→
   227→        let entries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(crate_dir)
   228→            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read directory: {}", crate_dir.display()))?
   229→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   230→            .filter(|e| {
   231→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   232→                e.path().is_file() && glob_pattern.matches(&name)
   233→            })
   234→            .map(|e| e.path())
   235→            .collect();
   236→
   237→        results.extend(entries);
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    // Remove duplicates and sort
   241→    results.sort();
   242→    results.dedup();
   243→
   244→    if results.is_empty() {
   245→        let pattern_list = patterns.join(", ");
   246→        bail!(
   247→            "no tspec files found matching '{}' in {}",
   248→            pattern_list,
   249→            crate_dir.display()
   250→        );
   251→    }
   252→
   253→    Ok(results)
   254→}
   255→
   256→/// Get binary path for a build with a spec
   257→pub fn get_binary_path(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec, release: bool) -> PathBuf {
   258→    let is_release = release || spec.cargo.profile.as_ref() == Some(&Profile::Release);
   259→
   260→    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {
   261→        spec.cargo
   262→            .target_json
   263→            .as_ref()
   264→            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))
   265→    });
   266→
   267→    let profile_dir = if is_release { "release" } else { "debug" };
   268→
   269→    match target {
   270→        Some(t) => workspace
   271→            .join("target")
   272→            .join(t)
   273→            .join(profile_dir)
   274→            .join(crate_name),
   275→        None => workspace.join("target").join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),
   276→    }
   277→}
   278→
   279→/// Get binary path for a simple build (no spec)
   280→pub fn get_binary_path_simple(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, release: bool) -> PathBuf {
   281→    let profile_dir = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
   282→    workspace.join("target").join(profile_dir).join(crate_name)
   283→}

   553→        fs::write(crate_dir.join(&opt_name), "# opt").unwrap();
   554→        fs::write(crate_dir.join("other.toml"), "# other").unwrap();
   555→
   556→        // Empty patterns = default "tspec*{SUFFIX}"
   557→        let found = find_tspecs(&crate_dir, &[]).unwrap();
   558→        assert_eq!(found.len(), 2);
   559→        assert!(
   560→            found

   563→        );
