     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace crates
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
     9→use crate::cargo_build::build_crate;
    10→use crate::run::run_binary;
    11→use crate::testing::test_crate;
    12→use crate::workspace::{CrateKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    13→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    14→
    15→/// Result of a batch operation on a single crate
    16→pub struct OpResult {
    17→    pub name: String,
    18→    pub success: bool,
    19→    pub message: String,
    20→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    21→}
    22→
    23→/// Build all workspace crates (excluding build tools)
    24→pub fn build_all(
    25→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    26→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    27→    release: bool,
    28→    strip: bool,
    29→    fail_fast: bool,
    30→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
    31→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    32→
    33→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
    34→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
    35→
    36→        let result = match build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
    37→            Ok(build_result) => {
    38→                if strip
    39→                    && member.has_binary
    40→                    && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
    41→                {
    42→                    eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
    43→                }
    44→                let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
    45→                OpResult {
    46→                    name: member.name.clone(),
    47→                    success: true,
    48→                    message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
    49→                    size,
    50→                }
    51→            }
    52→            Err(e) => OpResult {
    53→                name: member.name.clone(),
    54→                success: false,
    55→                message: e.to_string(),
    56→                size: None,
    57→            },
    58→        };
    59→
    60→        let failed = !result.success;
    61→        results.push(result);
    62→
    63→        if failed && fail_fast {
    64→            break;
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    results
    69→}
    70→
    71→/// Run all app crates sequentially
    72→pub fn run_all(
    73→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    74→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    75→    release: bool,
    76→    strip: bool,
    77→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
    78→    let mut results = Vec::new();
    79→
    80→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
    81→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
    82→
    83→        let result = match build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
    84→            Ok(build_result) => {
    85→                if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
    86→                    eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
    87→                }
    88→                match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
    89→                    Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
    90→                        name: member.name.clone(),
    91→                        success: true, // We don't treat non-zero exit as failure
    92→                        message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
    93→                        size: None,
    94→                    },
    95→                    Err(e) => OpResult {
    96→                        name: member.name.clone(),
    97→                        success: false,
    98→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
    99→                        size: None,
   100→                    },
   101→                }
   102→            }
   103→            Err(e) => OpResult {
   104→                name: member.name.clone(),
   105→                success: false,
   106→                message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   107→                size: None,
   108→            },
   109→        };
   110→
   111→        results.push(result);
   112→    }
   113→
   114→    results
   115→}
   116→
   117→/// Test all workspace crates
   118→pub fn test_all(
   119→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   120→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   121→    release: bool,
   122→    fail_fast: bool,
   123→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   124→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   125→
   126→    // Test regular crates (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   127→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   128→        if member.kind == CrateKind::Test {
   129→            continue; // Handle test crates separately
   130→        }
   131→
   132→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   133→
   134→        let result = match test_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
   135→            Ok(()) => OpResult {
   136→                name: member.name.clone(),
   137→                success: true,
   138→                message: "ok".to_string(),
   139→                size: None,
   140→            },
   141→            Err(e) => OpResult {
   142→                name: member.name.clone(),
   143→                success: false,
   144→                message: e.to_string(),
   145→                size: None,
   146→            },
   147→        };
   148→
   149→        let failed = !result.success;
   150→        results.push(result);
   151→
   152→        if failed && fail_fast {
   153→            return results;
   154→        }
   155→    }
   156→
   157→    // Handle test crates (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   158→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   159→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   160→
   161→        // Build the test crate (builds all binaries)
   162→        if let Err(e) = build_crate(&member.name, tspec, release) {
   163→            results.push(OpResult {
   164→                name: member.name.clone(),
   165→                success: false,
   166→                message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   167→                size: None,
   168→            });
   169→            if fail_fast {
   170→                return results;
   171→            }
   172→            continue;
   173→        }
   174→
   175→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   176→        let profile = if release { "release" } else { "debug" };
   177→        let target_dir = workspace.root.join("target").join(profile);
   178→
   179→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   180→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   181→            .into_iter()
   182→            .flatten()
   183→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   184→            .filter(|e| {
   185→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   186→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   187→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   188→                let is_executable = e
   189→                    .metadata()
   190→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   191→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   192→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   193→            })
   194→            .collect();
   195→
   196→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   197→            results.push(OpResult {
   198→                name: member.name.clone(),
   199→                success: false,
   200→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   201→                size: None,
   202→            });
   203→            continue;
   204→        }
   205→
   206→        for entry in test_binaries {
   207→            let path = entry.path();
   208→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   209→
   210→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   211→
   212→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   213→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   214→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   215→                        println!("ok");
   216→                        OpResult {
   217→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   218→                            success: true,
   219→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   220→                            size: None,
   221→                        }
   222→                    } else {
   223→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   224→                        OpResult {
   225→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   226→                            success: false,
   227→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   228→                            size: None,
   229→                        }
   230→                    }
   231→                }
   232→                Err(e) => {
   233→                    println!("FAILED");
   234→                    OpResult {
   235→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   236→                        success: false,
   237→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   238→                        size: None,
   239→                    }
   240→                }
   241→            };
   242→
   243→            let failed = !result.success;
   244→            results.push(result);
   245→
   246→            if failed && fail_fast {
   247→                return results;
   248→            }
   249→        }
   250→    }
   251→
   252→    results
   253→}
   254→
   255→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   256→pub fn print_test_summary(results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   257→    let max_name_len = results
   258→        .iter()
   259→        .map(|r| r.name.len())
   260→        .max()
   261→        .unwrap_or(5)
   262→        .max(5);
   263→
   264→    println!();
   265→    print_header!("TEST SUMMARY");
   266→    println!("  {:width$}  Status", "Crate", width = max_name_len);
   267→
   268→    let mut passed = 0;
   269→    let mut failed = 0;
   270→
   271→    for result in results {
   272→        let status = if result.success {
   273→            passed += 1;
   274→            "[PASS]"
   275→        } else {
   276→            failed += 1;
   277→            "[FAIL]"
   278→        };
   279→        println!("  {:width$}  {status}", result.name, width = max_name_len);
   280→    }
   281→
   282→    println!();
   283→    println!("  Test: {} passed, {} failed", passed, failed);
   284→    println!();
   285→    println!("  Note: Run `tspec test -p tspec` or `cargo test -p tspec` to test tspec itself");
   286→    print_hline!();
   287→    println!();
   288→
   289→    if failed > 0 {
   290→        ExitCode::from(1)
   291→    } else {
   292→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   293→    }
   294→}
   295→
   296→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   297→pub fn print_summary(results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   298→    let max_name_len = results
   299→        .iter()
   300→        .map(|r| r.name.len())
   301→        .max()
   302→        .unwrap_or(5)
   303→        .max(5);
   304→
   305→    println!();
   306→    print_header!("BUILD SUMMARY");
   307→    println!("  {:width$}  Status    Size", "Crate", width = max_name_len);
   308→
   309→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   310→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   311→
   312→    for result in results {
   313→        let status = if result.success {
   314→            ok_count += 1;
   315→            "[ OK ]"
   316→        } else {
   317→            failed_count += 1;
   318→            "[FAIL]"
   319→        };
   320→        let size_str = result
   321→            .size
   322→            .map(format_size)
   323→            .unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   324→        println!(
   325→            "  {:width$}  {status}  {:>6}",
   326→            result.name,
   327→            size_str,
   328→            width = max_name_len
   329→        );
   330→    }
   331→
   332→    println!();
   333→    println!("  Build: {} ok, {} failed", ok_count, failed_count);
   334→    print_hline!();
   335→    println!();
   336→
   337→    if failed_count > 0 {
   338→        ExitCode::from(1)
   339→    } else {
   340→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   341→    }
   342→}
   343→
   344→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   345→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   346→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   347→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   348→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   349→    } else {
   350→        format!("{}", bytes)
   351→    }
   352→}
   353→
   354→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   355→pub fn print_run_summary(results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   356→    let max_name_len = results
   357→        .iter()
   358→        .map(|r| r.name.len())
   359→        .max()
   360→        .unwrap_or(5)
   361→        .max(5);
   362→
   363→    println!();
   364→    print_header!("RUN SUMMARY");
   365→    println!("  {:width$}  Exit", "Crate", width = max_name_len);
   366→
   367→    let mut error_count = 0;
   368→
   369→    for result in results {
   370→        if result.success {
   371→            // Extract exit code number from message "exit code: X"
   372→            let code = result
   373→                .message
   374→                .strip_prefix("exit code: ")
   375→                .unwrap_or(&result.message);
   376→            println!(
   377→                "  {:width$}  {:>4}",
   378→                result.name,
   379→                code,
   380→                width = max_name_len
   381→            );
   382→        } else {
   383→            error_count += 1;
   384→            println!(
   385→                "  {:width$}  ERROR: {}",
   386→                result.name,
   387→                result.message,
   388→                width = max_name_len
   389→            );
   390→        }
   391→    }
   392→
   393→    println!();
   394→    if error_count > 0 {
   395→        println!("  Run: {} error(s)", error_count);
   396→    }
   397→    print_hline!();
   398→    println!();
   399→
   400→    if error_count > 0 {
   401→        ExitCode::from(1)
   402→    } else {
   403→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   404→    }
   405→}
   406→

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::process::Command;
     4→
     5→use crate::cargo_build::{apply_spec_to_command, generate_build_rs};
     6→use crate::find_paths::{find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec};
     7→use crate::tspec::load_spec;
     8→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
     9→fn requires_nightly(spec: &crate::types::Spec) -> bool {
    10→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
    11→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
    12→
    13→    let has_build_std = !spec.rustc.build_std.is_empty();
    14→
    15→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
    16→
    17→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
    18→}
    19→
    20→/// Test a crate with a spec
    21→pub fn test_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<()> {
    22→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    23→    let crate_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, crate_name)?;
    24→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&crate_dir, tspec)?;
    25→
    26→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    27→    let build_rs_path = crate_dir.join("build.rs");
    28→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    29→
    30→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo test
    31→    let status = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    32→        let spec = load_spec(path)?;
    33→        println!("Testing {} with spec {}", crate_name, path.display());
    34→
    35→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
    36→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    37→        if has_linker_args && !had_build_rs {
    38→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, crate_name, &spec)?;
    39→        }
    40→
    41→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    42→        if requires_nightly(&spec) {
    43→            cmd.arg("+nightly");
    44→        }
    45→        cmd.arg("test");
    46→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(crate_name);
    47→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    48→
    49→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, release)?;
    50→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?
    51→    } else {
    52→        println!("Testing {} (no tspec)", crate_name);
    53→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
    54→        cmd.arg("test");
    55→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(crate_name);
    56→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
    57→        if release {
    58→            cmd.arg("--release");
    59→        }
    60→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo test")?
    61→    };
    62→
    63→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
    64→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
    65→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    if !status.success() {
    69→        bail!("cargo test failed");
    70→    }
    71→
    72→    Ok(())
    73→}
    74→

     1→//! `tspec ts set` - Set a scalar value in a tspec
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→
     6→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     7→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     8→use crate::tspec::{load_spec, save_spec_snapshot};
     9→use crate::types::{OptLevel, PanicStrategy, Profile, Spec};
    10→
    11→/// Set a value in a tspec and save as versioned snapshot
    12→pub fn set_value(
    13→    project_root: &Path,
    14→    package: Option<&str>,
    15→    key: &str,
    16→    value: &str,
    17→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    18→) -> Result<()> {
    19→    let workspace = project_root;
    20→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    21→
    22→    // Load existing spec or use default
    23→    let mut spec = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    24→        Some(path) => load_spec(&path)?,
    25→        None => Spec::default(),
    26→    };
    27→
    28→    // Apply the key=value change
    29→    apply_value(&mut spec, key, value)?;
    30→
    31→    // Determine base name for snapshot
    32→    let base_name = match tspec {
    33→        Some(t) => {
    34→            // Strip suffix if present
    35→            t.strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    36→                .or_else(|| t.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    37→                .unwrap_or(t)
    38→                .to_string()
    39→        }
    40→        None => "tspec".to_string(),
    41→    };
    42→
    43→    // Save as versioned snapshot
    44→    let output_path = save_spec_snapshot(&spec, &base_name, &package_dir)?;
    45→
    46→    println!(
    47→        "Saved {}",
    48→        output_path
    49→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    50→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    51→            .display()
    52→    );
    53→
    54→    Ok(())
    55→}
    56→
    57→/// Apply key=value to a spec
    58→fn apply_value(spec: &mut Spec, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    59→    match key {
    60→        // Top-level high-level options
    61→        "panic" => {
    62→            spec.panic = Some(parse_panic_mode(value)?);
    63→        }
    64→        "strip" => {
    65→            spec.strip = Some(parse_strip_mode(value)?);
    66→        }
    67→
    68→        // Cargo config
    69→        "cargo.profile" => {
    70→            spec.cargo.profile = Some(parse_profile(value)?);
    71→        }
    72→        "cargo.target_triple" => {
    73→            spec.cargo.target_triple = Some(value.to_string());
    74→        }
    75→
    76→        // Rustc config
    77→        "rustc.opt_level" => {
    78→            spec.rustc.opt_level = Some(parse_opt_level(value)?);
    79→        }
    80→        "rustc.panic" => {
    81→            spec.rustc.panic = Some(parse_panic_strategy(value)?);
    82→        }
    83→        "rustc.lto" => {
    84→            spec.rustc.lto = Some(parse_bool(value)?);
    85→        }
    86→        "rustc.codegen_units" => {
    87→            spec.rustc.codegen_units = Some(
    88→                value
    89→                    .parse()
    90→                    .with_context(|| format!("invalid codegen_units: {}", value))?,
    91→            );
    92→        }
    93→
    94→        _ => bail!("unknown key: {}", key),
    95→    }
    96→
    97→    Ok(())
    98→}
    99→
   100→fn parse_panic_mode(s: &str) -> Result<PanicMode> {
   101→    match s {
   102→        "unwind" => Ok(PanicMode::Unwind),
   103→        "abort" => Ok(PanicMode::Abort),
   104→        "immediate-abort" => Ok(PanicMode::ImmediateAbort),
   105→        _ => bail!(
   106→            "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
   107→            s
   108→        ),
   109→    }
   110→}
   111→
   112→fn parse_strip_mode(s: &str) -> Result<StripMode> {
   113→    match s {
   114→        "none" => Ok(StripMode::None),
   115→        "debuginfo" => Ok(StripMode::Debuginfo),
   116→        "symbols" => Ok(StripMode::Symbols),
   117→        _ => bail!(
   118→            "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
   119→            s
   120→        ),
   121→    }
   122→}
   123→
   124→fn parse_profile(s: &str) -> Result<Profile> {
   125→    match s {
   126→        "debug" => Ok(Profile::Debug),
   127→        "release" => Ok(Profile::Release),
   128→        _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", s),
   129→    }
   130→}
   131→
   132→fn parse_opt_level(s: &str) -> Result<OptLevel> {
   133→    match s {
   134→        "0" => Ok(OptLevel::O0),
   135→        "1" => Ok(OptLevel::O1),
   136→        "2" => Ok(OptLevel::O2),
   137→        "3" => Ok(OptLevel::O3),
   138→        "s" => Ok(OptLevel::Os),
   139→        "z" => Ok(OptLevel::Oz),
   140→        _ => bail!("invalid opt-level: {} (expected: 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z)", s),
   141→    }
   142→}
   143→
   144→fn parse_panic_strategy(s: &str) -> Result<PanicStrategy> {
   145→    match s {
   146→        "abort" => Ok(PanicStrategy::Abort),
   147→        "unwind" => Ok(PanicStrategy::Unwind),
   148→        "immediate-abort" => Ok(PanicStrategy::ImmediateAbort),
   149→        _ => bail!(
   150→            "invalid panic strategy: {} (expected: abort, unwind, immediate-abort)",
   151→            s
   152→        ),
   153→    }
   154→}
   155→
   156→fn parse_bool(s: &str) -> Result<bool> {
   157→    match s {
   158→        "true" | "yes" | "1" => Ok(true),
   159→        "false" | "no" | "0" => Ok(false),
   160→        _ => bail!("invalid boolean: {} (expected: true/false)", s),
   161→    }
   162→}
   163→
   164→#[cfg(test)]

   166→    use super::*;
   167→

   170→        let mut spec = Spec::default();
   171→        apply_value(&mut spec, "strip", "symbols").unwrap();
   172→        assert_eq!(spec.strip, Some(StripMode::Symbols));
   173→    }
   174→
   175→    #[test]

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     2→use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
     3→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     4→
     5→use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;
     6→use crate::types::Spec;
     7→
     8→/// Load a spec from a TOML file
     9→pub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec> {
    10→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
    11→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
    12→    parse_spec(&content)
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Parse a spec from TOML string
    16→pub fn parse_spec(toml_str: &str) -> Result<Spec> {
    17→    toml::from_str(toml_str).context("failed to parse spec TOML")
    18→}
    19→
    20→/// Serialize a spec to TOML string (canonical form for hashing)
    21→pub fn serialize_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    22→    toml::to_string(spec).context("failed to serialize spec")
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Compute hash of a resolved spec (first 8 hex chars)
    26→pub fn hash_spec(spec: &Spec) -> Result<String> {
    27→    let toml_str = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    28→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();
    29→    hasher.update(toml_str.as_bytes());
    30→    let result = hasher.finalize();
    31→    Ok(hex::encode(&result[..4]))
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Save a spec to a TOML file, creating parent directories if needed
    35→pub fn save_spec(spec: &Spec, path: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    36→    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
    37→        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)
    38→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", parent.display()))?;
    39→    }
    40→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    41→    std::fs::write(path, &content)
    42→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Save a spec snapshot with sequence number and hash: `{name}-{seq:03}-{hash}.toml`
    46→/// Returns the path of the created file.
    47→pub fn save_spec_snapshot(spec: &Spec, name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {
    48→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)
    49→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", dir.display()))?;
    50→
    51→    // Find highest existing sequence number for this name
    52→    let prefix = format!("{}-", name);
    53→    let next_seq = std::fs::read_dir(dir)
    54→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read directory: {}", dir.display()))?
    55→        .filter_map(|entry| entry.ok())
    56→        .filter_map(|entry| {
    57→            let filename = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    58→            if filename.starts_with(&prefix) && filename.ends_with(TSPEC_SUFFIX) {
    59→                // Extract sequence number: {name}-{seq:03}-{hash}{TSPEC_SUFFIX}
    60→                let rest = filename.strip_prefix(&prefix)?;
    61→                let seq_str = rest.split('-').next()?;
    62→                seq_str.parse::<u32>().ok()
    63→            } else {
    64→                None
    65→            }
    66→        })
    67→        .max()
    68→        .map(|n| n + 1)
    69→        .unwrap_or(1);
    70→
    71→    let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
    72→    let filename = format!("{}-{:03}-{}{}", name, next_seq, hash, TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    73→    let path = dir.join(&filename);
    74→
    75→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
    76→    std::fs::write(&path, &content)
    77→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
    78→
    79→    Ok(path)
    80→}

     1→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

     4→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};

     6→/// Build profile - mutually exclusive
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8→#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
     9→pub enum Profile {
    10→    Debug,
    11→    Release,
    12→}
    13→
    14→/// Optimization level - mutually exclusive
    15→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    16→pub enum OptLevel {
    17→    #[serde(rename = "0")]
    18→    O0,
    19→    #[serde(rename = "1")]
    20→    O1,
    21→    #[serde(rename = "2")]
    22→    O2,
    23→    #[serde(rename = "3")]
    24→    O3,
    25→    #[serde(rename = "s")]
    26→    Os,
    27→    #[serde(rename = "z")]
    28→    Oz,
    29→}
    30→
    31→/// Panic strategy - mutually exclusive
    32→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    33→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    34→pub enum PanicStrategy {
    35→    Abort,
    36→    Unwind,
    37→    /// Nightly only: -C panic=immediate-abort (eliminates panic formatting)
    38→    ImmediateAbort,
    39→}
    40→
    41→/// Cargo-specific configuration (flat struct)
    42→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    43→pub struct CargoConfig {
    44→    /// Build profile (debug or release)
    45→    pub profile: Option<Profile>,
    46→    /// Target triple (e.g., "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl")
    47→    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    48→    /// Custom target JSON file path
    49→    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    50→    /// Nightly-only -Z flags (e.g., ["panic-immediate-abort"])
    51→    #[serde(default)]
    52→    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Rustc codegen and compilation configuration (flat struct)
    56→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    57→pub struct RustcConfig {
    58→    /// Optimization level (-C opt-level=)
    59→    pub opt_level: Option<OptLevel>,
    60→    /// Panic strategy (-C panic=)
    61→    pub panic: Option<PanicStrategy>,
    62→    /// Enable LTO (-C lto=true)
    63→    pub lto: Option<bool>,

    65→    pub codegen_units: Option<u32>,

    67→    #[serde(default)]
    68→    pub build_std: Vec<String>,

    73→

    76→pub struct VersionScript {

    78→    pub global: Vec<String>,

    80→    #[serde(default = "default_local")]

Adding `target_dir` to the `[cargo]` spec section creates per-spec subdirectories under `target/`, solving the collision.\n\nPath structure: `target/{target_dir}/{triple}/{profile}/{binary}` (using cargo's native `--target-dir`)\n\n## Steps\n\n### 1. Add `target_dir` field to `CargoConfig` (`src/types.rs:43`)\n\nAdd `pub target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig`. Serde handles `None` → omitted automatically.\n\n### 2. Add helpers in `src/tspec.rs`\n\nTwo new public functions:\n\n- `spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String` — strips `.ts.toml` suffix from filename\n- `expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>>` — expands `<name>` and `<hash>` placeholders; returns `None` if field is absent or empty\n\n### 3. Update `get_binary_path` in `src/find_paths.rs:257`\n\nAdd `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter. When `Some(td)`, base path becomes `workspace.join(\"target\").join(td)` instead of `workspace.join(\"target\")`. Update all 7 existing test call sites to pass `None`.\n\n### 4. Update `apply_spec_to_command` in `src/cargo_build.rs:128`\n\nAdd `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter. When `Some(td)`, insert `cmd.arg(\"--target-dir\").arg(workspace.join(\"target\").join(td))`. Also fix version script path (line 232) to use expanded target dir.\n\n### 5. Refactor `build_crate` in `src/cargo_build.rs:20`\n\n- Consolidate the double `load_spec` call (lines 34 and 42) into one\n- Compute `spec_name` and `expanded_td` after loading\n- Pass `expanded_td` to both `get_binary_path` and `apply_spec_to_command`\n- Add `target_base: PathBuf` to `BuildResult` (line 15)\n\n### 6. Update `test_crate` in `src/testing.rs:49`\n\nCompute `expanded_td` from loaded spec and pass to `apply_spec_to_command`.\n\n### 7. Fix test binary scanning in `src/all.rs:162-177`\n\nCapture `BuildResult` from `build_crate` (currently only error case captured). Use `build_result.target_base.join(profile)` instead of hardcoded `workspace.root.join(\"target\").join(profile)`.\n\n### 8. Add `cargo.target_dir` to `tspec ts set` (`src/ts_cmd/set.rs:58`)\n\nAdd match arm for `\"cargo.target_dir\"` in `apply_value`.\n\n### 9. Tests\n\n- Unit tests for `expand_target_dir` (None, empty, literal, `<name>`, `<hash>`, combined)\n- Unit tests for `spec_name_from_path`\n- Unit test for `get_binary_path` with target_dir\n- Unit test for `apply_value` with `cargo.target_dir`\n- Existing tests: append `None` to `get_binary_path` calls (mechanical)\n\n## Verification\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec\ntspec clippy\ntspec fmt --check\n```\n"
}
```

     1→# Chores-2
     2→
     3→Continued maintenance tasks, following on from chores-1.
     4→
     5→## 20260206 - Ts Execute Conversion
     6→
     7→### Convert `Commands::Ts` to use Execute trait
     8→
     9→The `Ts` variant in `Commands` is the only command that doesn't use the `Execute` trait. All other commands (Build, Run, Test, Clean, Clippy, Fmt, Compare, Version, Install) follow the pattern where a command struct implements `Execute` and main.rs calls `cmd.execute(&project_root)`. The `Ts` subcommands instead destructure fields in main.rs and call free functions from `ts_cmd::*`.
    10→
    11→**Current state in main.rs:**
    12→```rust
    13→Commands::Ts { command } => match command {
    14→    TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
    15→        ts_cmd::list_tspecs(package.as_deref(), all)?;
    16→    }
    17→    TsCommands::Show { package, all, tspec } => {
    18→        ts_cmd::show_tspec(package.as_deref(), all, tspec.as_deref())?;
    19→    }
    20→    TsCommands::Hash { package, all, tspec } => {
    21→        ts_cmd::hash_tspec(package.as_deref(), all, tspec.as_deref())?;
    22→    }
    23→    TsCommands::New { name, package, from } => {
    24→        ts_cmd::new_tspec(package.as_deref(), &name, from.as_deref())?;
    25→    }
    26→    TsCommands::Set { assignment, package, tspec } => {
    27→        let (key, value) = assignment.split_once('=')...;
    28→        ts_cmd::set_value(package.as_deref(), key, value, tspec.as_deref())?;
    29→    }
    30→},
    31→```
    32→
    33→**Goal state in main.rs:**
    34→```rust
    35→Commands::Ts(cmd) => {
    36→    cmd.execute(&find_project_root()?)?;
    37→}
    38→```
    39→
    40→**Implementation steps:**
    41→
    42→1. **Create `src/cmd/ts.rs`** with a `TsCmd` struct that wraps `TsCommands`:
    43→   ```rust
    44→   #[derive(Args)]
    45→   pub struct TsCmd {
    46→       #[command(subcommand)]
    47→       command: TsCommands,
    48→   }
    49→
    50→   impl Execute for TsCmd {
    51→       fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    52→           match &self.command {
    53→               TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
    54→                   ts_cmd::list_tspecs(package.as_deref(), *all)?;
    55→               }
    56→               // ... other variants
    57→           }
    58→           Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    59→       }
    60→   }
    61→   ```
    62→
    63→2. **Move `TsCommands` enum** from `cli.rs` to `src/cmd/ts.rs` (or keep in cli.rs and import — either works, but co-locating with the Execute impl is cleaner).
    64→
    65→3. **Update `cli.rs`** to use `Ts(TsCmd)` tuple variant instead of `Ts { command: TsCommands }` struct variant:
    66→   ```rust
    67→   // Before
    68→   Ts {
    69→       #[command(subcommand)]
    70→       command: TsCommands,
    71→   },
    72→   // After
    73→   Ts(TsCmd),
    74→   ```
    75→
    76→4. **Update `src/cmd/mod.rs`** to add `mod ts;` and `pub use ts::TsCmd;`.
    77→
    78→5. **Update main.rs** to replace the 30-line `Ts` match arm with the one-liner.
    79→
    80→6. **Consider whether `ts_cmd/` functions need `project_root`** — currently they call `find_project_root()` internally or use `current_dir()`. The Execute trait passes `project_root`, so the ts_cmd functions could be updated to accept it instead of finding it themselves. This is optional but would make them consistent.
    81→
    82→**Files affected:**
    83→- `src/cmd/ts.rs` (new)
    84→- `src/cmd/mod.rs` (add module + re-export)
    85→- `src/cli.rs` (change `Ts` variant, possibly move `TsCommands`)
    86→- `src/main.rs` (simplify match arm)
    87→- `src/ts_cmd/*.rs` (optional: accept `project_root` parameter)
    88→
    89→**Status:** Done
    90→

## Approved Plan:
# Add `cargo.target_dir` spec field

## The Problem

When two specs share the same target triple, builds overwrite each other in `target/{triple}/{profile}/`. This makes `tspec compare` unreliable and prevents side-by-side builds.

## The Design

Add a `target_dir` field to the `[cargo]` spec section with template placeholder support:

```toml
[cargo]
target_dir = "<name>"           # spec filename sans .ts.toml
target_dir = "<hash>"           # 8-char content hash
target_dir = "<name>-<hash>"    # combined
target_dir = ""                 # empty = no subdir (backward compat)
```

Path structure uses cargo's native `--target-dir`: `target/{target_dir}/{triple}/{profile}/{binary}`

Default when field absent: empty (backward compatible, no subdirectory).

## The Plan

### Context

The `target_dir` field threads through the build pipeline: types → spec loading → path resolution → cargo command → binary scanning. The double `load_spec` call in `build_crate` gets consolidated along the way.

### Steps

1. **Add `target_dir` field to `CargoConfig`** (`src/types.rs:43`)
   - Add `pub target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig`

2. **Add helpers in `src/tspec.rs`**
   - `spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String` — strips `.ts.toml` suffix from filename
   - `expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>>` — expands `<name>` and `<hash>` placeholders; returns `None` if field is absent or empty

3. **Update `get_binary_path`** (`src/find_paths.rs:257`)
   - Add `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter
   - When `Some(td)`, base path becomes `workspace.join("target").join(td)` instead of `workspace.join("target")`
   - Update all 7 existing test call sites to pass `None`

4. **Update `apply_spec_to_command`** (`src/cargo_build.rs:128`)
   - Add `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter
   - When `Some(td)`, insert `--target-dir target/{td}` into cargo command
   - Fix version script path (line 232) to use expanded target dir

5. **Refactor `build_crate`** (`src/cargo_build.rs:20`)
   - Consolidate the double `load_spec` call (lines 34 and 42) into one
   - Compute `spec_name` and `expanded_td` after loading
   - Pass `expanded_td` to both `get_binary_path` and `apply_spec_to_command`
   - Add `target_base: PathBuf` to `BuildResult` (line 15)

6. **Update `test_crate`** (`src/testing.rs:49`)
   - Compute `expanded_td` from loaded spec and pass to `apply_spec_to_command`

7. **Fix test binary scanning** (`src/all.rs:162-177`)
   - Capture `BuildResult` from `build_crate` (currently only error case captured)
   - Use `build_result.target_base.join(profile)` instead of hardcoded `workspace.root.join("target").join(profile)`

8. **Add `cargo.target_dir` to `tspec ts set`** (`src/ts_cmd/set.rs:58`)
   - Add match arm for `"cargo.target_dir"` in `apply_value`

9. **Tests**
   - Unit tests for `expand_target_dir` (None, empty, literal, `<name>`, `<hash>`, combined)
   - Unit tests for `spec_name_from_path`
   - Unit test for `get_binary_path` with target_dir
   - Unit test for `apply_value` with `cargo.target_dir`
   - Existing tests: append `None` to `get_binary_path` calls (mechanical)

### Verification

```bash
tspec test -p tspec
tspec clippy
tspec fmt --check
```

```

Design Motivation & Original Problem\n\nFrom **notes/chores-1.md** (lines 230-270), the `target_dir` feature addresses a critical issue:\n\n**Problem**: When using different specs with the same target triple, builds overwrite each other:\n```\ntspec build -t tspec.static-opt.ts.toml  → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/\ntspec build -t tspec.dyn-opt.ts.toml     → target/x86_64-unknown-linux-tspec/release/  (overwrites!)\n```\n\nThis occurs because both specs generate binaries to the same output directory despite having different compilation parameters, making it impossible to compare builds side-by-side without manual directory management.\n\n**Proposed Solution**: Add a `target_dir` field to the `[cargo]` section that supports template placeholder expansion to create separate output directories per spec.\n\n### 2. Current Architecture\n\n#### 2.1 Spec Types (`src/types.rs`)\n\nThe spec structure is organized in three main configuration sections:\n\n```rust\npub struct Spec {\n    pub panic: Option<PanicMode>,      // High-level panic mode\n    pub strip: Option<StripMode>,      // High-level strip mod

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tspec.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Save a spec to a TOML file, creating parent directories if needed",
  "new_string": "/// Extract spec name from path by stripping the .ts.toml (or .toml) suffix\npub fn spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String {\n    let filename = path\n        .file_name()\n        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string())\n        .unwrap_or_default();\n\n    filename\n        .strip_suffix(TSPEC_SUFFIX)\n        .or_else(|| filename.strip_suffix(\".toml\"))\n        .unwrap_or(&filename)\n        .to_string()\n}\n\n/// Expand template placeholders in a spec's target_dir field.\n/// Returns None if target_dir is absent or empty.\npub fn expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {\n    let raw = match &spec.cargo.target_dir {\n        Some(td) if !td.is_empty() => td,\n        _ => return Ok(None),\n    };\n\n    let mut expanded = raw.clone();\n\n    if expanded.contains(\"<name>\") {\n        expanded = expanded.replace(\"<name>\", spec_name);\n    }\n\n    if expanded.contains(\"<hash>\") {\n        let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;\n        expanded = expanded.replace(\"<hash>\", &hash);\n    }\n\n    if expanded.is_empty() {\n        Ok(None)\n    } else {\n        Ok(Some(expanded))\n    }\n}\n\n/// Save a spec to a TOML file, creating parent directories if needed"
}
```

assert_eq!(expand_target_dir(&spec, \"foo\").unwrap(), None);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn expand_target_dir_literal() {\n        let mut spec = Spec::default();\n        spec.cargo.target_dir = Some(\"my-subdir\".to_string());\n        assert_eq!(\n            expand_target_dir(&spec, \"foo\").unwrap(),\n            Some(\"my-subdir\".to_string())\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn expand_target_dir_name_placeholder() {\n        let mut spec = Spec::default();\n        spec.cargo.target_dir = Some(\"<name>\".to_string());\n        assert_eq!(\n {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/find_paths.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Get binary path for a build with a spec\npub fn get_binary_path(workspace: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec, release: bool) -> PathBuf {\n    let is_release = release || spec.cargo.profile.as_ref() == Some(&Profile::Release);\n\n    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {\n        spec.cargo\n            .target_json\n            .as_ref()\n            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))\n    });\n\n    let profile_dir = if is_release { \"release\" } else { \"debug\" };\n\n    match target {\n        Some(t) => workspace\n            .join(\"target\")\n            .join(t)\n            .join(profile_dir)\n            .join(crate_name),\n        None => workspace.join(\"target\").join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Get binary path for a build with a spec\npub fn get_binary_path(\n    workspace: &Path,\n    crate_name: &str,\n    spec: &Spec,\n    release: bool,\n    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,\n) -> PathBuf {\n    let is_release = release || spec.cargo.profile.as_ref() == Some(&Profile::Release);\n\n    let target = spec.cargo.target_triple.clone().or_else(|| {\n        spec.cargo\n            .target_json\n            .as_ref()\n            .and_then(|p| p.file_stem().map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().to_string()))\n    });\n\n    let profile_dir = if is_release { \"release\" } else { \"debug\" };\n\n    let base = match expanded_target_dir {\n        Some(td) => workspace.join(\"target\").join(td),\n        None => workspace.join(\"target\"),\n    };\n\n    match target {\n        Some(t) => base.join(t).join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),\n        None => base.join(profile_dir).join(crate_name),\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// Build a crate with a spec, returns the binary path on success\npub fn build_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n    let crate_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, crate_name)?;\n    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&crate_dir, tspec)?;\n\n    // Get actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when crate_name is a path)\n    let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&crate_dir)?;\n\n    // Track if we generated a build.rs\n    let build_rs_path = crate_dir.join(\"build.rs\");\n    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();\n\n    // Determine binary path based on spec\n    let binary_path = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {\n        let spec = load_spec(path)?;\n        get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, &spec, release)\n    } else {\n        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)\n    };\n\n    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build\n    let status = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {\n        let spec = load_spec(path)?;\n        println!(\"Building {} with spec {}\", pkg_name, path.display());\n\n        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed\n        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();\n        if has_linker_args && !had_build_rs {\n            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, &spec)?;\n        }\n\n        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(&spec)?;\n        cmd.arg(\"build\");\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, &spec, &workspace, release)?;\n        cmd.status().context(\"failed to run cargo\")?\n    } else {\n        println!(\"Building {} (no tspec)\", pkg_name);\n        let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(\"build\");\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n        if release {\n            cmd.arg(\"--release\");\n        }\n        cmd.status().context(\"failed to run cargo\")?\n    };\n\n    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)\n    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {\n        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);\n    }\n\n    if !status.success() {\n        bail!(\"cargo build failed\");\n    }\n\n    println!(\"  {}\", binary_path.display());\n    Ok(BuildResult { binary_path })\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Build a crate with a spec, returns the binary path on success\npub fn build_crate(crate_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let workspace = find_project_root()?;\n    let crate_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, crate_name)?;\n    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&crate_dir, tspec)?;\n\n    // Get actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when crate_name is a path)\n    let pkg_name = get_crate_name(&crate_dir)?;\n\n    // Track if we generated a build.rs\n    let build_rs_path = crate_dir.join(\"build.rs\");\n    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();\n\n    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir\n    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {\n        let s = load_spec(path)?;\n        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);\n        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;\n        (Some(s), td)\n    } else {\n        (None, None)\n    };\n\n    // Compute paths\n    let target_base = match &expanded_td {\n        Some(td) => workspace.join(\"target\").join(td),\n        None => workspace.join(\"target\"),\n    };\n    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, spec, release, expanded_td.as_deref())\n    } else {\n        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)\n    };\n\n    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build\n    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {\n        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();\n        println!(\"Building {} with spec {}\", pkg_name, path.display());\n\n        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed\n        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();\n        if has_linker_args && !had_build_rs {\n            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;\n        }\n\n        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec)?;\n        cmd.arg(\"build\");\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;\n        cmd.status().context(\"failed to run cargo\")?\n    } else {\n        println!(\"Building {} (no tspec)\", pkg_name);\n        let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(\"build\");\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n        if release {\n            cmd.arg(\"--release\");\n        }\n        cmd.status().context(\"failed to run cargo\")?\n    };\n\n    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)\n    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {\n        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);\n    }\n\n    if !status.success() {\n        bail!(\"cargo build failed\");\n    }\n\n    println!(\"  {}\", binary_path.display());\n    Ok(BuildResult {\n        binary_path,\n        target_base,\n    })\n}"
}

> AGENT

The only difference is `args = ["-static"]` vs `args = [""]`. Both use the custom target JSON with `build_std`.

This isn't a tspec bug — it's a linking issue. `-static` forces fully static linking against glibc, and statically-linked glibc binaries are notorious for segfaulting because glibc's `dlopen`/NSS/iconv internals don't work correctly when statically linked. The dynamic build works because glibc is loaded normally.

A few thoughts:

1. **glibc + `-static` = broken** — glibc was never designed for full static linking. Things like `getaddrinfo`, locale, and thread-local storage can segfault.

2. **If you want static linking**, you'd typically use `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` as the target triple instead of a glibc-based target. musl is designed for static linking.

3. **The existing todo item [11]** ("Investigate `-static` vs `dynamic-linking=false` size difference") is related — `-C link-arg=-static` and `-C prefer-dynamic=false` behave differently precisely because of this glibc problem.

This is outside tspec's scope — tspec is faithfully passing `-static` to the linker as requested. The fix is either switching to musl for static builds or removing `-static` from the spec.

     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It allows configuring builds via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping.
     8→
     9→## Build Commands
    10→
    11→Ensure a recent tspec is installed:
    12→```bash
    13→cargo install --path .         # Install from local source
    14→```
    15→
    16→Development workflow:
    17→```bash
    18→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests
    19→tspec clean                    # Clean build artifacts
    20→tspec clippy                   # Run lints
    21→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting
    22→```
    23→
    24→## Architecture
    25→
    26→### Execute Trait Pattern
    27→
    28→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    29→
    30→```rust
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    37→```
    38→
    39→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    40→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    41→
    42→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    43→```rust
    44→enum Commands {
    45→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    46→    Test(TestCmd),
    47→    // ...
    48→}
    49→```
    50→
    51→### Key Modules
    52→
    53→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    54→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, execute_cargo_subcommand helper, re-exports
    55→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    56→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    57→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    58→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing
    59→- `cargo_build.rs` - Build orchestration with spec application
    60→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace member discovery
    61→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    62→
    63→### Translation Spec Structure
    64→
    65→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    66→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    67→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    68→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    69→
    70→## Conventions
    71→
    72→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    73→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    74→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-crate projects; tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    75→
    76→## Workflow
    77→
    78→**Before committing, run verification:**
    79→```bash
    80→tspec test -p tspec
    81→tspec clippy
    82→tspec fmt --check
    83→```
    84→
    85→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
    86→```
    87→Committed abc123.
    88→
    89→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
    90→```
    91→
    92→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
    93→
    94→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
    95→
    96→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
    97→
    98→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
    99→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   100→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   101→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   102→
   103→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   104→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/notes/README.md",
  "old_string": "Todo.md contains two main sections \"Todo\" and \"Done\" each item is a\nshort explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more\nreferences.\n\nExamples:\n\n# Todo\n- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)\n- Fix bug Y [1]\n\n# Done\n- Fixed issue Z [2,3]\n\n[1]: bugs.md#bug-y\n[2]: issues.md#issue-z\n[3]: fixes.md#fix-z",
  "new_string": "Todo.md contains two main sections \"Todo\" and \"Done\" each item is a\nshort explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more\nreferences.\n\nMultiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.\nIn markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`\nis parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).\n\nExamples:\n\n# Todo\n- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)\n- Fix bug Y [1]\n\n# Done\n- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]\n\n[1]: bugs.md#bug-y\n[2]: issues.md#issue-z\n[3]: fixes.md#fix-z"
}
```
